For two bars the two are alike on the meter; for the next two the spread one is really 3 dB down, and for the last two 6 dB: somewhere on the way the two should sound alike. In each pair of bars the order is one, four, four, one. Nobody has heard it.

**v38 (chirper-zero): THE ODD ONE OUT SEEMS LONGER.** In bars 185 to 192 (5:53.3), the last row with the drum, a plain note on every beat, a sixteenth after the drum: 32 notes, every one held the same 300 ms. All are C5 but four (the 6th, 13th, 20th and 27th: every seventh, so never twice in the same place of the bar), which are G4, a fourth below. Nothing else about those four is different. The one that is not like its fellows is said to seem longer than they are (the oddball effect: Tse, Intriligator, Rivest and Cavanagh, 2004). Nobody has heard it.

## t6 Small Change (chirper-one)

**The rule (the coin problem).** Almost nothing is written: a bass drum, a bass, and one SEED, a single struck note every bar or two (100 of them in the song). Everything else is change given for it. A pair of COINS is two echoes, p and q sixteenths long, that feed themselves and each other; every pass through the p coin lifts the sound a fifth, every pass through the q coin drops it a fourth. So an echo passed a times through p and b times through q lands `p*a + q*b` sixteenths after its seed, sounds `7*a - 5*b` semitones from it (one step round the circle of fifths per coin spent, whichever coin), and is heard on the side of its last coin (p left, q right). In the generator: `PAIRS`, `UP`, `DOWN`, `G`, `lattice`, `frobenius`.

**What cannot be made.** With coins of 3 and 5 the amounts 1, 2, 4 and 7 cannot be made and every amount from 8 on can (the largest that cannot is `p*q - p - q`): a seed is answered by a few separate notes (sixteenths 3, 5, 6) and then by a roll. Coins of 3 and 4 fill from 6 on; coins of 5 and 7 leave gaps up to 23. Anyone may borrow a pair of coins for an echo that fills by itself.

**Five fifths.** From a seed on B flat the first five steps are B flat, F, C, G, D: G minor's five notes, with nobody choosing them. Seeds on E flat and F move the harmony by moving one note.

**v1 (chirper-one, the skeleton): SMALL CHANGE.** 122, G minor, 192 bars (6:18), twelve sections of sixteen: one seed (coins 3 and 5, a seed every four bars, alone); the drum; the bass; threes and fours; both purses (a seed a bar, the pairs in turn); fives and sevens (no drum, a seed every four bars); the drum again; three purses; off the beat (the seed on the off-beat of beat two, so the whole lattice moves); the same a tone up; home; the last seed. Read on the first seed: the echoes sound within 0.9 semitone of the lattice out to sixteenth 21; the first two are 2 dB under the seed, sixteenths 6 to 10 another 6 to 12 dB down, and the roll from 11 on 12 to 30 dB under the first two; they peak between 14 ms before and 23 ms after their sixteenths (the pitch effect is late and not evenly so). Score 96.12 on both seeds. The sections with no drum are 11 to 12 dB under the rest. Not heard by its composer.

**v2 (chirper-one): THE COINS DO MORE THAN ECHO.** Three switches (`--without-hat`, `-debts`, `-till`; all three off is v1 byte for byte) and one left off for an ear (`--with-denser`). (1) THE HAT PLAYS WHAT CAN BE PAID: counting sixteenths from where the seed falls, a hat on every amount the section's two coins can make, softer the more coins it takes. With 3 and 5: `. . . X . X x . x - x - , - , -`; with 3 and 4: `. . . X X . x x x - - - - , , ,`; with 5 and 7: `. . . . . X . X . . x . x . x -`. The bar fills up like a till, and each pair has its own pattern. With the drum (bar 17) come the two coins alone; with the bass (33), everything they can pay. (2) THE BASS PLAYS WHAT CANNOT: the amounts no number of the two coins makes (3 and 5: 1 2 7; 3 and 4: 1 2 5; 5 and 7: 1 2 3 6 9 11 13; never with the drum), the largest of them a tone lower when it is in the bar. Hat and bass never share a sixteenth. (3) In the two sections where the seed is off the beat (129 to 160) the hat and the bass are counted from sixteenth 6 as well: the whole till stands six sixteenths round from the drum and comes home with the seed. (4) THE TILL: a low-pass inside each purse's two loops sweeps between 1.5 and 10 kHz once in p x q sixteenths (15, 12, 35), the time after which both coins' echoes of one seed first land together; seed and coins alone carry 11 dB less above 4 kHz in the median sixteenth. LEFT OFF: a seed every bar in the opening and the break. Rendering the seam from Hearing Things showed two bars in every four of the opening are silent (under -56 dB); filled, the scorer stops counting the drum's entry as a section (88.3 against 96.6). Whether that silence is a pause or a hole is for an ear. 96.60 on both seeds (v1 today 96.12): the top end, 10.4 dB short at 14.8 kHz, is inside 5 dB everywhere. Not heard by its composer.

**v3 (chirper-one): WHAT IS GIVEN BACK.** Four switches (`--without-long`, `-turn`, `-chord`, `-wait`; all four off is v2 byte for byte). (1) LONG: in the opening and the break a seed came every four bars and its change was gone in two. Those eight seeds now go into a second pair of loops that give back 1.05 of every pass where the others give back 0.62: more than they are given, and they still run out, because every pass also moves the note until it is out of hearing. The opening reads -18 -21 -27 -34 dB a bar where it read -18 -36 -56 -81; the seeds are 6 dB down, since at full level the limiter held the seed and the opening stood as loud as the drum. (2) TURN: where the seed is off the beat (bars 129 to 160) the drum gives up beats two and four, so the seed falls between two drums. (3) WAIT: in the two bars before home (159, 160) the drum, the bass and the hat stop and the last off-beat seed's change is paid out alone (-19, -25 dB against -13). (4) CHORD: home is a chord. From bar 161 the seed is G minor (G4 B flat 4 D5); every coin moves a note a fifth, so the purse walks it round the fifths, D minor, A minor, each quieter: the only chords in the song. 96.60 on both seeds (v2 under the album's seven cuts 94.23): home now counts at every cut (0.27), seven sections at all seven. The drum's entry at bar 17 no longer counts (0.12, was 0.19): the opening is not silent before it. The turn at 129 still reads under 0.10. Not heard by its composer.

**v4 (chirper-one): THE PURSE INSIDE OUT.** The turn (bars 129 to 160, the seed off the beat) is the song's idea and was its quietest moment. Three switches (`--without-leave`, `-flip`, `-fill`; all three off is v3 byte for byte). (1) LEAVE: the drum waits whenever the seed changes its place in the bar, to leave as well as to come home: in bars 127 and 128 the drum, the bass and the hat stop and the three purses pay out alone. (2) FLIP: while the seed is off the beat the coins change hands (a fourth purse, `d`: 5 and 3). The five lifts a fifth on the left and the three drops a fourth on the right. The same amounts can be made, so the hat and the bass do not change; the quick coin's run now falls in fourths under the seed where it climbed in fifths over it. Read on the purse alone after an E flat 4 seed: B flat 3 on the right at 3 sixteenths, B flat 4 on the left at 5, F3 right at 6, F4 in the middle at 8, F5 left at 10. At home the purse is the right way out again. (3) FILL: the break's long purse (5 and 7) gives back 1.35 a pass where the opening's gives 1.05, so the break's fourth bars read -38 to -44 dB where they read -54 to -60; alone after its last seed it falls every bar and is gone in nine. 98.69 and 98.76 on two seeds (v3 96.60): the turn counts on the scorer for the first time (0.26) and there are eight sections at every cut. Not heard by its composer.

**v5 (chirper-one): SMALLER CHANGE.** One switch (`--without-smaller`; off is v4 byte for byte) and one that ships off (`--with-inside`). SMALLER: the song ended as it began, thinner: four seeds, each with the change the first seed had. Now each of the last four seeds (bars 177, 181, 185, 189) is paid in smaller coins than the one before: 3 and 5 sixteenths as ever, 3 and 5 thirty-seconds, 3 and 5 sixty-fourths, and then 3 and 5 of a unit so small it is a pitch, the period of B flat 3 (4.29 ms). At that size the coin problem is heard all at once: the amounts that cannot be made (1, 2, 4, 7) are a rattle nine milliseconds long, and from 8 on every amount can be made, which is an echo every 4.29 ms, which is B flat. The last seed's change is its own note, held (B flat 4, 2 cents sharp, four bars in going): the smallest change there is gives you back what you paid. No pitch effect in that purse: it is the pitch. Anyone may borrow it: sounds.md has the recipe. INSIDE (off): the second purse inside out (4 and 3) in the second half of its two sections; it works by solo and the scorer takes 1.45 points for it, so it waits for the ear's answer on the turn at bar 129. 98.97 and 99.02 on two seeds (v4 98.69). Not heard by its composer.

**v6 (chirper-one): SMALL CHANGE FIRST.** The ending pays its last seeds in smaller and smaller coins, and until then nothing in the song had heard a small coin; and every new section but two simply began. Four switches (`--without-roll`, `-gap`, `-tumble`, `-lone`; all off is v5 byte for byte). (1) ROLL: on beat three of the last bar before a section, a seed on the next section's first note, paid in coins of 3 and 5 thirty-seconds where the purse stays the same and 3 and 5 sixty-fourths where it is new: the song's own lattice at two and four times the speed. Six where the drum plays (bars 32, 48, 64, 80, 112, 144); none where the drum already waits, none before the last section, which lays the small coins out in order and goes one size further. By solo it is a flurry a beat long that runs out (4 to 6 dB less each half beat), not a roll that builds. (2) GAP: the drum gives beat four of that bar to the change (five bars; in the others there is no drum on four). (3) LONE: the same before the drum first comes and before it comes again (bars 16 and 96), where the roll is alone; there it is struck 10 dB softer, and in the finished song reads -18 dBFS on its beat like the lone seeds round it (any lone sound goes to the master's ceiling). (4) TUMBLE: 'off the beat, up' (bars 145 to 160) was 'off the beat' again a tone higher; now the purse turns over at every seed, inside out, the right way, inside out, so the quick coin's run falls in fourths on the right, then climbs in fifths on the left (by solo the first echo is 8 dB to the right, then 8 to the left, in turn). 99.62 on both seeds (v5 98.97), and the points are the scorer's: the only term that moves is the share of the song it hears as returning, 0.709 to 0.703 against a band that stops at 0.70 (a build with no lone rolls read 0.715 and 98.41). Not heard by its composer.

**v7 (chirper-one): INTEREST.** Two switches (`--without-grow`, `-owed`; both off is v6 byte for byte). (1) GROW: v6's small change before a section could only run out (a purse gives back less than it is given), so the roll was loudest on its seed and fell away into the one. Now the roll's purse pays interest: its two loops give back more than they are given, so the change grows pass on pass for the two beats it has, and a hand on the purse (its fader, which its own echoes go through too) shuts it on the one: a roll that builds and is cut off by the drum. By solo, by quarter beat from the stroke to the one: -38 -42 -46 -38 -36 -36 -31 -29 in thirty-seconds where v6 fell -26 to -41, and nothing after the one. (2) OWED: the peak was not the loudest section, because the bass plays what this bar's purse cannot pay and two of the peak's three purses have few debts. Where purses share a section (both purses, three purses) the bass also plays what the other purses there cannot pay, a third higher, on B flat 2. At the peak that is the same nine strokes in every bar (1 2 3 5 6 7 9 11 13) and which of them are low says whose turn it is; and there, for the first time, the bass and the hat meet, since what one purse can pay another cannot. By solo the bass is 3.4 dB up at the peak; the section reads -11.7 for -12.7 and is still 0.4 under 'the drum again'. 100.00 on both seeds (v6 99.62): that is the scorer's band edge, not a perfect song (the share it reads as returning went from 0.703 to 0.667 across 0.70). Not heard by its composer.

**v8 (chirper-one): PAID IN FULL.** Two switches (`--without-zeno`, `-meet`; both off is v7 byte for byte). (1) ZENO: v7's roll pays interest and is shut on the one, which is right where the drum is playing and wrong in the two bars where there is none (16 and 96, before the drum first comes and before it comes again): alone, a roll that grows ended 1 dB under the drum it was there to bring in. There the change is now paid in coins that shrink: after the seed each gap is three quarters of the one before and each coin that much softer, so eight coins and all the coins after them fit into the two beats that are left, the last on the one, which is the drum: a debt paid in full in endless instalments. The song's two coins take turns: odd instalments a fifth up on the left, even ones a fourth down on the right, so the roll is the fifth of the next section's first note, an octave apart, closing on it. No loop: one echo a coin, at 1 - 0.75^k of the way. (2) MEET: since v7 the bass plays the other purses' debts where purses share a section, and there the bass and the hat fall together for the first time (what one purse cannot pay another can). Now the hat says so: where it meets a B flat in the bass it is let ring (`sc-open`, a new sound, over the shut one). At the peak that is six open hats in a bar of threes and fives or threes and fours and two in a bar of fives and sevens. 100.00 on both seeds, as v7: the same band edge, not a perfect song; the scorer hears none of it. Not heard by its composer.

**v9 (chirper-one): THREE FOR TWO.** Three switches (`--without-exchange`, `-rate`, `-seven`; all three off is v8 byte for byte). Every purse in the song counted in sixteenths, or in halves and quarters of them: one currency. (1) EXCHANGE: at home (bars 161 to 176: the chord and the first purse, nothing new since v2) the same seed is also paid in another: a purse with the same coins, 3 and 5, of a unit two thirds of a sixteenth (three of its units for two sixteenths, six to the beat). Its 3 is an eighth, so every amount that is a multiple of three lands on the song's grid and every other amount (5, 8, 10, 11, 13 ...) on the triplet between: the same lattice, the same fifths up on the left and fourths down on the right, the same largest debt (7), at one and a half times the speed, three against two with the first purse. A hand opens it for home only. (2) RATE: the hat plays what the first purse can pay, in sixteenths; at home a second hat on the other side plays what the exchange can pay, in its units, where that falls between the first hat's sixteenths (5, 8, 10 and 11 of them after each chord), softer the more coins an amount takes. Two hats counting the same sums in two currencies. (3) SEVEN: the bass plays what cannot be paid, and the largest debt on F; at home it plays the exchange's largest debt too, 7 of its units after each chord (a beat and a sixth): the one bass note in the song that is off the grid. 100.00 on both seeds, as v8: the scorer hears none of it. Not heard by its composer.

**v10 (chirper-one): PAID ABROAD.** Three switches (`--without-abroad`, `-herald`, `-count`; all three off is v9 byte for byte). (1) ABROAD: the bass plays what the first purse cannot pay (1, 2, 4 and 7 sixteenths). But two sixteenths are three of the exchange's units, and three it can pay: at home, after each chord, that debt is paid in the other currency and the bass does not play it; the exchange's first coin sounds alone where the bass's G was (in v9 it fell on that note and the finished song read 3 dB lower there). One and seven sixteenths are one and a half and ten and a half units: nobody pays those, and the bass keeps them. (2) HERALD: the two bars before home are the wait (only what is left of the last off-beat seed). On beat three of the second a seed is given to the exchange alone: 3, 5, 6, 8, 9, 10, 11 of its units, and 12 is the one of home. From 8 on every sixth of a beat is paid, so the change before home is a roll in the other currency that fills in as it comes: the exchange is heard on its own before it is heard against the first purse. (3) COUNT: the second hat counts that roll, a stroke on each amount, softer the more coins: seven strokes closing up into sixths. 100.00 on both seeds, as v9: the scorer hears none of it. Not heard by its composer.

**v11 (chirper-one): THREE AND FIVE MAKE EIGHT.** The song's sum has been in its echoes since v1 and nowhere in its notes. Two switches (`--without-spell`, `-bought`; both off is v10 byte for byte). (1) SPELL: home's chord (G, B flat, D on the one, every two bars) is counted out in home's second half (bars 169 to 176): G on the one, B flat three sixteenths on, D five after that, on sixteenth 8, which is beat three: the chord is paid in the purse's own two coins, and its top note falls where the two coins first meet (3 + 5 = 5 + 3, the one amount the purse pays twice over). On that beat the D is struck over the purse's A (the G's eight) and F (the B flat's five): D minor for one beat, in G minor. (2) BOUGHT: and the drum gives that beat away: it rests on beat three of bars 169, 171, 173 and 175. And once before, so that it is a rule: when the drum comes back after the break the purse is fives and sevens, and 5 + 7 = 12 is beat four: the drum rests on beat four of bars 105, 107, 109 and 111. Least Common gives beats to its answer and Wind Down to its meetings; here a beat is bought, for the sum of the purse. 99.99 and 99.99 on two seeds (v10 re-read 100.0 and 100.0): kept 0.006 under. Not heard by its composer.

**v12 (chirper-one): FIVE AND THREE MAKE EIGHT TOO.** Two switches (`--without-back`, `-rings`; both off is v11 byte for byte). v11 put the song's sum into its notes once: the right way round, on a beat. (1) BACK: home's chord is struck whole twice (bars 161, 163) and counted out in its second half (G, B flat, D on sixteenths 0, 3, 8: three, then five). Between the two (bars 165, 167) it is now counted out the other way, from the top down: D, B flat, G on sixteenths 0, 5, 8: five, then three. Home is the chord, the purse inside out, and the purse; both spellings end on the same sixteenth because 5 + 3 is 3 + 5. (2) RINGS: where the purse's coins first meet ON a beat the drum gives the beat away (v11). In two sections they meet off the beat and the drum has nothing to give: threes and fours (3 + 4: sixteenth 7) and off the beat (5 + 3 from sixteenth 6: sixteenth 14). There, in the second half, the hat on the sum is let ring three sixteenths in each bar that has a seed (bars 57 to 63 and 137 to 143, four each). On a beat the sum is a rest; off a beat it is a ring. A new patch name, sc-rung (the open hat built first was only a louder stroke). 99.99 and 99.99 on two seeds (v11 re-read 99.99 and 99.99): no score in it; kept on its merits. Not heard by its composer.

**v13 (chirper-one): THE OTHER CURRENCY HAS ITS OWN COIN.** Two switches (`--without-coin`, `-through`; both off is v12 byte for byte). Since v9 home is also paid in another currency (three of its units to two sixteenths) and a second hat ticks what that purse can pay where it falls between the sixteenths. It was the first hat's own patch on the other side: the same sound in the same band. (1) COIN: the second hat is a patch of its own, `sc-coin`: the first hat at two thirds, as its unit of time is two thirds of a sixteenth: 20 ms for 30, and the centre of its sound 5.5 kHz for 8.3 (under the first hat, where the song has nothing else). (2) THROUGH: it counts the exchange's change through the whole bar (to 24 of its units) and not two beats of it: twelve ticks for four, each softer by the coins it took, so that for one bar in two home is heard cut in twenty-four against the hat's sixteen: three for two, to the bar line. 99.98 on both seeds (v12 re-read 99.99): 0.005 under, kept. Not heard by its composer.

**v14 (chirper-one): THE HAT RINGS ON THE LARGEST DEBT.** One switch (`--without-let`; off is v13 byte for byte). Since v8 the hat is 'let ring' where what one purse pays is what another owes; v12 found it never rang (a second stroke on the other side, 140 ms). Now, where the debt it meets is another purse's LARGEST (the largest amount that purse cannot make: 5 for threes and fours, 7 for threes and fives: the number the bass already marks with its low note), the hat really rings: `sc-rung`, three sixteenths and stopped. In both purses that is all sixteen: sixteenth 5 in the first purse's bars, 7 in the second's, bar about. In three purses it is the same two places (5, 7, and both in the third purse's bars): eighteen of sixty-eight; the other fifty stay strokes. The figure learnt in bars 65 to 80 comes back at the peak. No stroke added: 34 moved from one hat to the other, each 6 dB under the track's other rings so that the ring starts where the old stroke stood. 99.97 on both seeds (v13 re-read 99.98): 0.016 under, kept. Not heard by its composer.

**v15 (chirper-one): THE BASS IS HEARD COMING.** Two switches (`--without-bare`, `-octave`; both off is v14 byte for byte). The third section is called 'the bass', and its entrance was the song's weakest boundary (it sat on the scorer's highest cut). (1) BARE: the bar before the bass comes (bar 32), the drum waits: only the hat's two strokes and the small change are left, and then the bass and the drum arrive in an empty band together (chirper-zero's rule: a quiet part in an empty band takes the arrival from whoever enters that band next; WAIT does the same before home). (2) OCTAVE: the bass has its octave (`sc-debt`: the library's sub-sine with its second harmonic 9 dB under the note in place of 19, and a low-pass at 300 Hz), so that what cannot be paid is heard from 175 to 233 Hz as well as from 87 to 117, on any speaker, on all its 536 notes. Measured: the entrance reads 0.24 on the scorer's novelty curve for 0.18 (both seeds); bar 32 finished -52 dBFS from 60 to 130 Hz for -19; the nine sections with the bass 2.3 to 4.9 dB up from 150 to 260 Hz and 0.2 to 0.3 down from 60 to 130. Two builds: the first (octave 6 dB under, no low-pass) took 0.6 dB off the low end. 99.93 on two seeds (v14 re-read 99.97): 0.03 under and kept: the song's worst band (71 Hz) is 0.3 dB shorter. Not heard by its composer.

**v16 (chirper-one): THE BASS STANDS ON ITS SEED'S KEY NOTE.** Two switches (`--without-root`, `-follow`; both off is v15 byte for byte). The song's rule already said it: a seed and the first four coins spent on it are five notes a fifth apart, and from B flat those are G minor's five, with their key note three coins on (B flat, F, C, G). The bass has stood on that G for every seed; but the form's seeds are also E flat and F, whose five notes are C minor's and D minor's. (1) ROOT: where a section has one seed the bass stands three coins on from it: 'off the beat' (seed E flat, bars 129 to 144) on C2, 'off the beat, up' (seed F, bars 145 to 160) on D2, home on G2 as ever: the song's last third is the fourth, the fifth and home, where it was G for six minutes. Its largest debt (a tone under) and what the other purses owe move with it. (2) FOLLOW: in 'the drum again' (bars 97 to 112: seeds E flat and F by turns) the bass follows each seed: two bars on C2, two on D2, and the peak begins on G. C2 and D2 are 65 and 73 Hz, between the drum's two partials (49 and 98), where this song had nothing. Measured, finished: from 55 to 80 Hz those three sections came up 6 to 8 dB and from 90 to 130 Hz went down as much; no note is added or moved in time (202 of 536 change pitch); each switch was rendered alone (99.998 and 100.0). 100.0 on both seeds (v15 re-read 99.93): 0.07 over, the spectrum term (71 Hz 5.0 dB short for 6.7). Not heard by its composer.

**v17 (chirper-one): THE BASS FOLLOWS EVERY SEED.** Four switches (`--without-comes`, `-both`, `-three`, `-lift`; all four off is v16 byte for byte). v16 found the bass's note in the rule (three coins on from the seed) and used it in three sections; in three more the seeds change and the bass was still on G under seeds whose notes are C minor's and D minor's. (1) COMES: 'the bass' (bars 33 to 48): it comes in on G2 for two bars and goes down to C2 for two, four times: the first thing the bass does is move. (2) BOTH: 'both purses' (bars 65 to 80): a bar on G2, a bar on D2: two purses, two notes. (3) THREE: 'three purses' (the peak, bars 113 to 128): three purses, three notes, a cycle of three bars against the four; from its NINTH bar: the peak stands on G for eight bars and then turns C2 G2 D2 C2 G2 D2 C2 G2. The first build turned from the peak's first bar and the peak stopped being an arrival (the curve at its start 0.18 for 0.22, a section lost, 99.51 for 100.0). (4) LIFT: wherever the bass is under its written note a second track plays it an octave up, 9 dB under (294 notes): for a speaker that has neither 65 nor 131 Hz. No note is added or moved in time by the first three: 92 of the bass's 536 notes change pitch. Measured, finished: from 55 to 80 Hz the three sections have 2.3 to 2.8 dB more and the bass's band moves bar by bar as written; the lift gives the low sections 4.4 to 4.8 dB from 120 to 160 Hz, nothing to speak of above 200 Hz, and costs the song 0.3 dB at both ends. 100.0 and 100.0 on two seeds (v16 re-read 100.0): level, kept for the ear. Not heard by its composer.

**v18 (chirper-one): A COIN IS SPUN ON THE COUNTER.** Two switches (`--without-spun`, `-lands`; both off is v17 byte for byte). The song's effects so far are sums (amounts made of coins); this is a coin's own motion. A coin spun on a table rattles faster and faster without limit and then stops dead (Euler's disc: a finite-time singularity); measured, its rattle's rate goes as (t0 - t)^(-1/3). The clicks are written from that law alone: beginning at the song's sixteenths and ending four bars later, click k of N falls at T (1 - (1 - k/N)^(3/2)), N = 96 for the 64 sixteenths it runs over: the rate is unbounded, the count finite. The gap goes from a sixteenth (123 ms) to 8 ms. It is the second hat's patch (`sc-coin`), left and right by turns as an escapement's pallets, the two sides closing to the middle as the coin's tilt does, (t0 - t)^(2/3). (1) SPUN: through the last four bars of the break (93 to 96): it stops dead on the one of bar 97, where the drum comes back. (2) LANDS: through the last four bars of home (173 to 176), over the whole band: it starts in step with the hat's sixteenths and runs away from them, and stops dead on the last section's first seed, as the drum and the bass leave. Measured: alone, all 96 written clicks found to 0.1 ms, left less right 5.2 dB closing to 0.3, nothing 30 ms after the stop; finished, in the break the coin's band is 15, 7, 3 and 17 dB over what was there by bar, at home 2, 5, 3 and 11 dB over the hats. 100.0 and 100.0 on two seeds (v17 re-read 100.0): level; under the ceiling the drum's return reads 0.56 for 0.41, likeness to Least Common is 0.695 for 0.675 and the song is 0.2 to 0.3 dB lighter under 1.5 kHz against the album. Not heard by its composer.

**v19 (chirper-one): THE COIN IS METAL.** Three switches on (`--without-metal`, `-damped`, `-flick`; all off is v18 byte for byte). v18's spun coin was the second hat's hiss: a hat speeding up. (1) METAL: it has a sound of its own (new patch `sc-spun`): a thin plate's modes from 4.2 kHz up, struck near the edge, with a short click on its front. (2) DAMPED: a table damps a coin more the flatter it lies: each rattle's ring lasts two gaps to the next and no more (245 ms at the first, 17 ms at the last): the ring obeys the law the rattle quickens by, fills the same share of every gap, and the coin stops dead. (3) FLICK: a coin does not start by itself: one stroke of the same metal in the middle on the spin's first instant, let ring. Measured: alone, the second half of every gap stands 16 to 20 dB under its rattle's front all the way (the old hiss: nothing between; one fixed ring: nothing at first, a smear at the end) and the 30 ms after the stop are 37 dB under the 30 ms before; in the finished song, above 4 kHz, the break's coin is 5.2 to 1.3 dB more by bar, its gaps 17 to 20 dB under the rattles (v18 43 to 48), the flick 18 dB over what was there; the 30 ms before each stop are 3 to 4 dB quieter than v18's: its cost. 100.0 and 100.0 on two seeds, as v18: the total cannot say; in the form the coin's start is a counted turn now (0.25 for 0.14: nine sections at every cut, two from the structure edge for one), recurrence share 0.571 for 0.642, likeness to Least Common 0.680 for 0.698. Not heard by its composer.

**v20 (chirper-one): THE COIN COUNTS THE CHANGE.** One switch on (`--without-tally`; off is v19 byte for byte). The break (fives and sevens, bars 81 to 92) was twelve bars with three written notes; the song's subject is which amounts can be made of two coins, and there nobody said it but the echoes. Now after each of the break's first three seeds the coin (v19's metal, a 50 ms clink) is put down for every amount the purse can pay: 5 7 10 12 14 15 17 19 20 21 22, and from 24 on every sixteenth (23 is the largest that cannot be made of fives and sevens): scattered clinks that become a run, counted further each time (to 24, to 30, to 35: the first amount made two ways, struck on both sides). Fives alone on the left, sevens alone on the right, both in the middle; 2.5 dB quieter with each coin. The fourth seed's coin is flicked and spun. And once more at the end, for the last section's first two seeds (bars 177, 181), in threes and fives (3 5 6, and from 8 every one), in the coins each seed is paid in: sixteenths, then thirty-seconds. Measured in the finished song: above 4 kHz the bar after each seed reads -40 dBFS where nothing was (-57); a clink's highest sample -10 to -26 dBFS at the amounts that can be made, the amounts that cannot as they were (-55); the bars whole within 0.3 dB; both ends within 0.2 dB. Its weak point is its level: the master lifts the break 17 dB. 100.0 and 100.0 on two seeds, as v19: the cap; the scorer saw the recurrence share fall (0.551 for 0.571) and three turns soften (the coin's start 0.20 for 0.25: two hundredths over the highest cut). Not heard by its composer.

**v21 (chirper-one): THE FIRST CHANGE IS COUNTED TOO, AND THE LAST SEED'S PURSE STAYS OPEN.** Two switches on (`--without-reckon`, `-linger`; both off is v20 byte for byte). The coin that counts the change in the break and at the end now counts it in the first section too, where the rule is first stated: after the seeds in bars 9 and 13 a clink on every amount threes and fives can pay (3 5 6, then every one from 8), to 16 and then to 24, with a worn coin (the spun coin through four low-passes at 7 kHz: Hearing Things' last bars lie over these and nothing may be added over 10 kHz). And the last section's first seed (bar 177) goes into the long purse, as the song's first four do: its change rings through the three bars after it, which were silent; the silence before Out of Step stays. Measured: a clink reads -21 to -33 dBFS from 4 to 8 kHz at every amount that can be paid and nothing changes at 1, 2, 4 and 7; bars 9 to 16 by bar are within 0.1 dB of v20's whole; over 10 kHz they have 0.2 dB more. Bars 178 to 180 read -22.0 -30.3 -39.2 dBFS for -35.4 -59.9 -84.9; bars 185 to 192, under Out of Step, -21.06 for -21.09. 100.0 and 100.0 on two seeds, as v20 (the cap). Not heard by its composer.

**v22 (chirper-one): THE CHANGE IS ROUNDED TO THE COIN.** One switch on (`--without-rounded`; off is v21 byte for byte). A till with no small coins rounds what it owes: the purse of the two sections off the beat (coins of 5 and 3, bars 129 to 160) has the engine's bit crusher last in each of its two loops, so its change is rounded to 32 amounts at every payment and stops owing instead of fading. In off the beat only the amount is rounded; in off the beat, up the time too (each sample held longer: 12 kHz sliding to 3 kHz), beside the other purse, which is still paid in full. At home nothing is rounded. Measured: the purse's level does not move (within 0.1 dB); what rounding changed stands 10 dB under the purse alone and 15 to 16 dB under the whole song, and from 1 to 4 kHz is as much as everything the old song had there; with the time rounded it rises to 13 dB under the song. The gaps between strokes are emptier: this purse's tails were those sections' air. Both ends as v21's. 100.0 and 100.0 on two seeds (the cap, as v21): kept by judgment. Not heard by its composer.

**v23 (chirper-one): THE BAR IS PAID.** Three switches on (`--without-paid`, `-cannot`, `-late`; all three off is v22 byte for byte). The purses pay the seeds' change; now a wooden key pays the bar itself. Sixteen sixteenths in threes and fives is 3 3 5 5, in six orders: the key is pressed once for each coin, where the coin begins, a different order each bar until all are used. Each coin moves its note as it moves the purse's change (up a fifth, down a fourth), so the notes are the same whatever the order (B flat, F, C, G): one way through the change, an octave over the seed, where the purse's echoes are every way at once. Fives and sevens cannot make sixteen: they pay twenty-four, a bar and a half, three payments to two bars, and the bar left over (112) is the amount that till cannot pay: the key rests. Off the beat it pays from the seed's sixteenth. Bars 49 to 80 and 97 to 160: 369 strokes. Measured (the build less v22, 640 to 2000 Hz, by sixteenth): in a bar with no seed the key stands 17 to 32 dB over the rest of its band off the beat; in a bar with one it is 6 dB under the seed on the one and 10 to 13 under the purse's first echoes, then level; on the drum's beats within 5 dB. Its strokes arrive over a range of 15 dB, not trimmed. Two builds not kept: an octave under the seed (10 to 15 dB under the drum on the beat) and at the seed's octave (the seed over again). Likeness to Least Common 0.703 (0.697). Both ends as v22's. 100.0 and 100.0 on two seeds, the scorer's cap, as v22: kept on judgment; the scorer saw next to nothing. Not heard by its composer.

**v24 (chirper-one): THE TILL CLOSES AT HOME, AND GIVES A RECEIPT.** Three switches on (`--without-offkey`, `-closing`, `-receipt`; all three off is v23 byte for byte). The key rested at home; now a payment to each seed there (bars 161 to 171): coins of 3 and 5 pay sixteen in six orders, six seeds, each order once more. A 3 moves the key's note up a fifth and a 5 down a fourth, so two of each is a major third up whatever the order: on the bar line where each payment ends the till gives a receipt, one stroke on the note its coins come to: D6 all six times, home's fifth. And v23's fault is mended: off the beat, where no seed is sown with it, a payment's first stroke is 9 dB down. Measured (the build less a build without the key, stroke by stroke, 440 to 2400 Hz): the trimmed strokes read -15.5 dBFS for -6.5; at home a payment's first stroke is 5.7 to 11.5 dB under its seed, and the receipts -12.6 to -13.4 dBFS, from 1.1 over to 7.3 under what else is on their bar lines. Both ends as they were to 0.1 dB. 100.0 and 100.0 on two seeds (v23 100.0 and 100.0): the cap; the scorer sees nothing in it; kept on judgment. Not heard by its composer.

**v25 (chirper-one): THE PURSE HAS A CLASP.** Three switches on (`--without-clasp`, `-miss`, `-snap`; all three off is v24 byte for byte). The song had no backbeat. A purse has a clasp: two small metal balls, click-CLICK (`sc-clasp`: G7 30 ms before the beat, D7 on it), snapped on beats 2 and 4 from bar 25 to bar 176 where the drum has not given the beat to the purse, and only where the purse can pay the beat: where the sixteenths since the section's last seed are an amount its coins make. With 3 and 5 a seed every two bars buys beat 4 of its own bar (12) and both beats of the next (20, 28) and not beat 2 of its own (4 is a debt): half a backbeat, then a whole one, by turns; with 3 and 4 every beat is bought; off the beat the drum plays one and three and the clasp has two and four to itself. Where the purse cannot pay, the clasp does not catch: the first ball only. And the hand that shuts the purse of small change on the one after every roll is heard: a snap on the one of bars 17 33 49 65 81 97 113 145. Measured (the build less v24, each ball's top from 1500 to 8000 Hz): the second ball -11.5 to -15.5 dBFS on the drum, the first 6 to 8 dB under it, a miss -16.4 to -19.4, the snap on the one -10.8 to -13.2; off the beat -12.6 and -13.4 to -15.9. Written 6 dB under, the first ball came out as loud as the second (this master lifts a click 30 ms before the drum 21 to 22 dB and one on it 10 to 12): it is written 14 under. The quiet pages are 0.06 dB up. 100.0 and 100.0 on two seeds (v24 100.0 and 100.0): the cap; kept on judgment. The sections and every cut are v24's; the likeness to Least Common went from 0.684 to 0.708. Not heard by its composer.

**v26 (chirper-one): THE TILL MULTIPLIES.** Three switches on (`--without-product`, `-melts`, `-hands`; all three off is v25 byte for byte). Every effect here so far adds coins; a ring modulator multiplies, and the product of two tones is their difference and their sum (sin 3x sin 5x is half of cos 2x less cos 8x). Before the seed of bars 5, 9 and 13 of each section from the drum to home (24 times; not off the beat) the purse's two coins sound as notes, the seed's own note three octaves down times each coin (F3 and D4 for a seed on B flat 4 and coins 3 and 5), for as many sixteenths as the two make, ending as the seed is sown; and as they sound they are multiplied, each by the other (`sc-product-35`, `-34`, `-57`): over the first coin's length they melt into their difference and their sum, which ring for the second's. 3 x 5 gives 2 and 8: B flat 2 and B flat 4, the seed's own note; 3 x 4 gives 1 and 7 (the seventh harmonic, a note no scale here has); 5 x 7 gives 2 and 12. The coins come one from each side; the product stands in the middle. Measured (the build less v25, rms from 50 to 900 Hz, all 24 notes on two seeds): the product's part -20.8 to -22.1 dBFS where the drum and bass play plainly, 3 to 5 dB under them in that band; at each note's end the coins are 37 to 91 dB under their difference and sum; the first coin 7.7 dB to the left, the second to the right, the product the same on both. At -16 dB it was a lead; at -24 it was hardly lower and both ends of the song were 0.8 dB lower (the loudness target): it is at -38. Both ends 0.06 dB lower. 100.0 and 100.0 on two seeds (v25 100.0 and 100.0); kept on judgment. One section count moved (10 to 9); every cut and recurrence as they were. Not heard by its composer.

**v27 (chirper-one): A DEBT HAS A TERM.** Two switches on (`--without-term`, `-settled`; both off is v26 byte for byte) and one written and off (`--with-chest`). The bass plays what the purse cannot pay, every debt a sixteenth long, the largest a tone under the rest. Now, in the bar its seed is sown, the largest debt is owed as long as it is large: seven sixteenths for a debt of seven, five for five (or until the next debt, or the section's end), 6 dB under the short notes: 68 held notes; and when its term is up it is paid: the bass is back on the key note for a sixteenth (47 notes). The bass has a line, two bars long: two short notes, a long one a tone under, and the step back. Held in every bar it overfilled the lows, moved both ends over a dB and put recurrence on its ceiling: in the seed's bar only, none of that. A bell on the drum at 71 Hz was built and left off (0.5 dB there for 0.6 dB off both ends). Measured: by band against the album 71 Hz is 2.1 dB short (it was 4.6: the song's worst band) and 83 to 133 Hz within 0.7 (they were 1.5 to 2.8 short); between the drum's strokes the low band reads -18.0 dBFS where it read -43.9, 6.6 dB under the short notes. Both ends 0.2 to 0.3 dB lower through the loudness target. 100.0 and 100.0 on two seeds, as v26; recurrence 0.571, as it was; the nearest song Least Common 0.694 (0.711). Not heard by its composer.

## t7 Out of Step (chirper-zero)

**One pattern against itself.** The half-bar pattern `x x . x . x x .` (sixteenths 0, 1, 3, 5, 6 of eight), on a click that is five cycles of a saw on G3. Click A plays it on the grid, a little left; click B plays the same thing a little right, phi sixteenths ahead: every B stroke sounds when `t + phi(t)` reaches it. The eight locks (X both, a or b one of them): 1 ahead `X a b a b X a b`; 2 `a X . X b a X b`; 3 `X a b X . X X .`; 4 `a X b a b X a b`; 5 `X X . X b a X .`; 6 `X a b X . X a b`; 7 `a X b a b a X b`; 8 is 0. Anyone may borrow the pattern or a lock.

**The escapement.** In "slips" phi goes up one a row: `k + u^2` over the row's first two bars (it creeps, then runs), then it CATCHES on the next sixteenth and holds for six. In "drifts" it runs back from 8 to 0 in a straight line, a sixteenth every four bars, through every lock without stopping.

**The comb.** As B leaves a lock, strokes that were together are 1, 3, 4, 7, 9, 14, 19 ms apart: two copies of one click that close cancel at the odd multiples of 1 / (2 gap). No effect is used.

**The long arc.** One low-pass on both clicks whose opening is the phase itself: `4000 * 2^(1.7 * phi / 8)` Hz.

**The bass that pumps itself.** Three pairs of sines on G2 (98 Hz) and its second and third harmonics, each pair 2.1 Hz apart: one cycle a beat at 126. Struck on the off-beat (step 2) and held for the bar, each pair agrees on the off-beats and cancels on the beats.

**v1 (chirper-zero, the skeleton): OUT OF STEP.** 126, G, 160 bars, 5:05. One click (A; B joins it at bar 8 and is not heard as a second thing; the bass drum at 16); slips (eight rows, eight catches; the fifth row has no bass drum); home; drifts; one again. Measured: together, A alone and B alone differ by 64 dB under one; in the first slip their sum reads 10 to 20 dB down at the predicted notches and 6 dB up at the predicted peaks; the bass alone is 18.5 dB lower on the beats than off them; the master's top (over 6 kHz against 1-3 kHz, where there is no hat) climbs 17, 15, 13, 11, 9 dB under at locks 0 to 4 and is 18 under when they are together again. 93.41 / 93.37. Not heard by anyone.

**v2 (chirper-zero): THE MOULD.** The pattern has three holes in its eight sixteenths: 2, 4 and 7. From "home" (2:48) a third click, C (`os-comp`: small, bright, on the grid), plays the holes and nothing else: `. . x . x . . x`, the pattern's complement. With A and B together the two interlock and every sixteenth of the bar sounds exactly once (`X X c X c X X c`): that is what home is. In "drifts" B's strokes fall into the holes C is filling, and B and C strike together on 3, 2, 1, 3, 1, 2, 3 of C's strokes as B passes 7, 6, 5, 4, 3, 2, 1 ahead; only at 0 do they never meet. (At four ahead B is the complement and two more: it covers every hole by itself.) Anyone may borrow the complement as this record's "fits only when home". In the generator: `COMP`, `c_notes`, `lock()` (which now prints `c` for C alone and `*` for B and C together); `C11T7_COMP=0` is v1 byte for byte. Measured on A, B and C alone at home: all sixteen sixteenths sound, the pattern's within 1 dB of each other and C's 9 to 10 dB under them. 94.95 / 94.92 (v1 re-read today: 94.01). Not heard by anyone.

**v3 (chirper-zero): THE TUNE THE TWO MAKE.** Wherever A and B strike on the same sixteenth the two clicks are one louder click, and those doubles are a different figure at every lock. A small glass pip (`os-tune`) plays exactly the doubles, and each stroke of the pattern has a note: G4, B flat 4, D5, F5, A5 on steps 0, 1, 3, 5, 6 (the record's chord: t1's G minor seventh with t2's ninth on top). So every lock has its own tune, found in the two clicks and not written: `G . . . . F . .` at one ahead, `. Bb . D . . A .` at two, `G . . D . F A .` at three, `. Bb . . . F . .` at four, `G Bb . D . . A .` at five, `G . . D . F . .` at six, `. Bb . . . . A .` at seven, and AT HOME THE WHOLE CHORD, `G Bb . D . F A .`. In "slips" the first four locks are bare; from the fifth (1:54) each lock is heard bare for two bars and then with its tune; the whole chord arrives at 2:40. In "drifts" each tune is heard only for the two bars in which B passes its lock, all seven backwards. Anyone may borrow it: `doubles(k)` and `TUNE`. `C11T7_TUNE=0` is v2 byte for byte. Measured: at locks 5 to 8 the two clicks alone are loudest (within 3 dB; the singles are 5 to 7 dB down) on exactly the sixteenths the pip alone strikes, 4 of 4, and in "drifts" the pip's strokes are the passing lock's doubles in 16 of 16 bars; its pitches read within 8 Hz of the notes. 95.07 / 95.05 (v2 re-read today: 94.70). Not heard by anyone.

**v4 (chirper-zero): THE PENDULUM.** A fourth kind of phase, and the smallest: under a millisecond. While the two clicks are one click and there is no bass drum (0:15 to 0:30, and 4:34 to 4:57), A is given to the left ear alone and B to the right, at the same level, and B swings 0.6 ms ahead of A and 0.6 ms behind it, there and back every two bars. A lead that small is not two clicks and not a rhythm: it is a PLACE. The one click swings from ear to ear like the pendulum of the clock the record is named after, and nothing is panned. THE DRUM HOLDS THE PENDULUM: at the start it stops dead on the bar the drum comes in; at the end the drum's last stroke lets it go and it swings to rest, half as far every three bars. On one speaker the same lead is a comb: a hollow at 833 Hz that opens and closes in the click twice a swing. Anyone may borrow it: `swing`, `lead_ms`, `apart_lane`, `click_pan`, `PEND_MS`, `PEND_BARS`. `C11T7_PEND=0` is v3 byte for byte. Measured on the two clicks alone: over all 200 strokes the right ear's lead is within 20 microseconds of the written one (a sample is 21), and the two ears' levels within 0.4 dB; summed to one speaker the stroke at the end of a swing is 20 dB down at 730 to 940 Hz. 94.47 / 94.41 (v3 re-read today: 94.83). NEEDS TWO EARS (headphones) for the swing. Not heard by anyone.

**v5 (chirper-zero): THE BASS HEARS THE LOCK.** The bass has held one G for the whole record. But every lock has a first stroke the two clicks share (the first note of its tune), and it is one of two: the pattern's first stroke, G, or its second, B flat. In "drifts", where B does not stop and nothing says where the locks are, the bass now does: as B passes each lock it plays the note the two agree on first. After sixty-six bars on G it moves (3:22): B flat four bars (the seventh lock), G eight (the sixth, the fifth), B flat four (the fourth), G four (the third), B flat four (the second), G home (4:08). Nobody wrote the line; the pattern's rotations put the notes where they are, and the bass is always the tune's lowest note (under B flat the tune has only B flat, D, F, A: a B flat major seventh; under G it always has G). The B flat is a second bass (`os-bass-b`) with its own ratios, so it pumps on the beat as the G does. Anyone may borrow it: `bass_lock`, `bass_note`, `ROOT_IN`. `C11T7_ROOT=0` is v4 byte for byte. Measured: in all 112 bars the bass plays, the louder of 98.0 and 116.5 Hz in the master is the written note (the other is 24 to 29 dB under it); alone, the B flat bass is 16.0 dB quieter on the beats than between them (the G: 15.8) and its lowest pair reads 116.55 and 118.65 Hz. 94.84 / 94.76 (v4 re-read under the ensemble: 94.25). Tried and refused: the bass following in "slips" too (92.10: thirteen sections that keep returning); in "slips" alone 94.56. Not heard by anyone.

**v6 (chirper-zero): THE TUNE OUT OF STEP WITH ITSELF.** At home the tune is the whole chord (G Bb . D . F A ., twice a bar) and has stood still. In home's second row (3:03 to 3:18) the tune is given a B of its own: a second pip, an octave lower, the same five notes, which does to the tune what B did to the click. It sets off as one with the tune, creeps ahead for half a bar (u squared, as B slips), catches a sixteenth ahead and holds; two bars on it slips again: four locks in eight bars. Each is a different piece of music from the same five notes twice: one ahead, every sixteenth sounds and the two run up the chord and back in each other's gaps (G+Bb Bb D D F F+A A G); two ahead, G Bb+D . D+F A F A+G Bb; three ahead, G+D Bb F D+A . F+G A+Bb .; four ahead (half a loop), G Bb+F A D G F+Bb A D. Where the click's phase made rhythms the tune's makes harmony, all of it inside the record's chord (Piano Phase's way; the click's is Clapping Music's). It stops when B lets go. Anyone may borrow it: `canon_phase`, `canon_notes`, `canon_lock`, `CANON_*`. `C11T7_CANON=0` is v5 byte for byte. Measured: in the file each lock's bar holds exactly the notes above; in what v6 adds to the render the loudest of the five notes is the written one at all 83 strokes, each starting within 4 ms of where it is written (median 1.25); it stands 9 dB under everything else on average (20 under on the bass drum, 1 over where it is alone); outside those eight bars v6 is v5 (50 dB under the music). 95.05 / 94.98 (v5 today 95.01 / 94.94). The scorer does not notice it. Not heard by anyone.

**v7 (chirper-zero): THE LOCKS HAVE A SHAPE.** For the first four rows of "slips" (bars 25 to 56, 0:46 to 1:47), where the locks are heard bare, A is given the left ear and B the right (0.85 of the way; each keeps a seventh of itself in the other ear). Nothing is moved while the two are together: two copies of one click, one in each ear at the same instant, are one click in the middle, and each click is turned up by what its far ear no longer gives (3.14 dB), so the two ears added are what they were. Each lock is now a PICTURE: the strokes the two share are in the middle, A's own on the left, B's own on the right (one ahead: 0,5 / 1,3,6 / 2,4,7; two ahead: 1,3,6 / 0,5 / 4,7; three ahead: 0,3,5,6 / 1 / 2; four ahead: 1,5 / 0,3,6 / 2,4,7). And every slip shows what phase is from the smallest up: as B leaves a lock its copy of a shared stroke reaches the right ear a fraction of a millisecond before A's reaches the left and the middle click LEANS right; at a millisecond it is on the right; by five a wide click; by thirty two clicks; by a sixteenth the next lock: a place, a side, a width, a flam, a rhythm, one number growing (v4's pendulum is the first of the five, held still). From the fifth row the clicks stand where they always stood and the tune, in the middle, names the strokes the picture showed there. The clicks are on their own tracks for those bars (`a-ear`, `b-ear`). Anyone may borrow it: `shape_span`, `shape_gain`, `eared`, `SHAPE_*`. `C11T7_SHAPE=0` is v6 byte for byte. Measured on the master (above 2 kHz): at one ahead the shared strokes read right less left -1 and 0 dB, A's own -16, B's own +15 to +16 (v6: -4 and +3); at three ahead the four shared within 1 dB, the others -16 and +16. In the first slip the lag at which the right ear best matches the left is 0.10, 1.04, 2.88, 4.12, 7.33, 9.25 ms where B's lead is written 0.11, 1.04, 2.88, 4.14, 7.33, 9.26 (v6: 0). A alone, the two ears added, is v6's within 0.01 dB (61 dB under the click), and its room is the same to 0.01 dB. On one speaker the whole master is not quite v6: those rows are 0.24 dB quieter and the rest 0.07 to 0.15 dB louder (the master's compressor answers a click that is in one ear), and what is left after that is 23 dB under the music in those rows. 95.14 / 95.14 (v6 today 95.05 / 94.98); the scorer's cut at 1:48 is at 0.180, on its top threshold. Refused: the picture from the first slip to the end of the drift (94.88 / 94.94 an ear each; 94.38 / 94.34 at 0.85): that cut slid to 0.167 and the form lost a section on two of seven cuts. Not heard by anyone; it needs two ears or two speakers.

**v8 (chirper-zero): HALF A WAVE OUT OF STEP.** The song is two clicks out of step by sixteenths, and what changes is the RHYTHM. The click itself is five turns of a wave (a saw on G3: 5.1 ms a turn). Put B ahead of A by HALF A TURN (2.55 ms) and what changes is the NOTE: every odd harmonic of the one meets itself upside down in the other and is gone, the even ones stay, and what is left is a click on the G an octave above. Put B a WHOLE turn ahead (5.1 ms) and the two waves are in step again though the clicks are not: the low G is back. It is the song in small: out of step by half, inside out; by the whole, home. In the last four bars of "one click" (bars 21 to 24, 0:38 to 0:46: after the pendulum, with the bass drum, before the first slip) the two clicks stand in one place (a click and its copy must reach the same ear to cancel) and B is half a turn ahead for two bars, a whole turn for two. Nothing is played but the same click twice. It is heard on ONE speaker: it is the one-speaker half of the pendulum (a lead too small to be a rhythm is a place to two ears, and a colour or a note to one). Own tracks for bars 17 to 24 (`a-mid`, `b-mid`). Anyone may borrow it: `wave_span`, `wave_ms`, `wave_gain`, `mid`, `WAVE_*`. `C11T7_WAVE=0` is v7 byte for byte. Measured, the two clicks alone and added: B's lead reads 2.552 and 5.102 ms (written 2.552, 5.100); at half a turn the odd harmonics (3 to 15) are 27.7 dB down and the even ones (4 to 16) unchanged (0.0 dB), the third against the fourth -32.9 dB where it was +1.3, the click's strongest period 2.54 ms (393 Hz) where it was 5.08 (197 Hz), and the click is 3.0 dB quieter; at a whole turn odd and even are back within 0.2 dB and the period is 5.10 ms. In the master, a stroke with no drum under it reads odd less even -28.6 and -24.0 dB in bars 21 and 22 (v7 +2.1, +2.5) and +1.8 in bars 23 and 24 (v7 the same). Bars 17 to 20 are v7's on one speaker (the same level to 0.1 dB); bars 21 and 22 come out 0.6 dB LOUDER and 23 and 24 0.3 dB (the master's compressor has less click to answer); the rest of the song is v7 to 62 to 65 dB. 95.13 / 95.13 (v7 today 95.15 / 95.15). Refused: a bar each, alternating, from bar 18 (94.81 / 94.78: the scorer's cut at the drum's entry fell from 0.152 to 0.125). Not heard by anyone.

**v9 (chirper-zero): THE WAVE'S OWN SCALE.** v8 found that B half a turn of the click's wave ahead of A is the click an octave up. That is the first rung of a ladder. Strike the SAME click n times, each a n-th of a turn (5.102 ms / n) after the last: every harmonic that is not a multiple of n meets n - 1 copies of itself spread evenly round the circle and cancels; the multiples of n arrive in step. What is left is the click on n times its note, no higher in peak than one click (n copies of a saw a n-th of a turn apart ARE a saw n times as high). So one click, with no other sound, no filter and no change of pitch, has a scale: 1 G3, 2 G4, 3 D5, 4 G5, 6 D6, 7 F6 (the natural seventh, 31 cents under the piano's), 8 G6, 9 A6 (5 is a B natural and is left out: the tune has B flat). Every one is a note-name of the song's own tune (G, D, F, A); the wave has no B flat to give. It is played in the second row of "one again" (bars 145 to 152, 4:34 to 4:49), where the drum has gone: a click on every beat, up the ladder in two bars and down it in two, twice; the last is one click again. Tracks `wave-1` … `wave-9` (the k-th copy of each stroke), in the middle, on `os-click`, -6 dB a copy, the clicks' own low-pass and room. Anyone may borrow it: `scale_span`, `scale_strokes`, `scale_notes`, `SCALE_*`. `C11T7_SCALE=0` is v8 byte for byte. Measured, the nine tracks alone: on every one of the 32 strokes the strongest harmonic of G3 is the rung's own (392, 588, 784, 1176, 1372, 1568, 1764 Hz); what should cancel is 12 to 16 dB under what should not (the click dies as it rings, so a later copy is smaller than the one it should cancel; and the engine starts a note on a whole sample); the peak FALLS 3 dB from the bottom rung to the top and the level over 45 ms RISES 6.4 dB (the click's own 500 Hz high-pass takes most of one click's two lowest harmonics and nothing from the higher rungs). In the master the scale is 16 to 19 dB under the music, and every other bar of the song is v8 to 72 dB with no gain change (0.002 dB), the seams included. 95.22 / 95.21 (v8 today 95.16 / 95.16): variety. Not heard by anyone.

**v10 (chirper-zero): A NOTE IS A CLICK IN STEP WITH ITS OWN WAVE.** The record's click is five turns of a wave (a saw on G3, 5.102 ms a turn). Strike it again before it has died and the second stroke falls on the first one's tail. A ROLL does that: the click on its own track, from sixteenths (8.4 strokes a second), faster by the same ratio every beat (1.69 times) for six beats, until the strokes are ONE TURN OF THE WAVE apart: 196 a second. There every stroke is in step with the tail of the one before, the tails add, and the row of clicks is one unbroken note, G3, held two beats to the bar line. On the way the rate is heard as a rhythm, then a buzz, then a pitch; and the strokes pass in and out of step with their own tails (in step a whole number of turns apart: 49 a second, G1; 65, C2; 98, G2). Once: bars 81 and 82 of "slips" (2:32 to 2:36), the two bars where the drum, the clap and the hat rest, landing on their return. Each stroke is turned down by the square root of the rate, so the roll gives the same power at every speed. Track `roll`, patch `os-click`, 357 strokes. Anyone may borrow it: `roll_strokes`, `roll_spans`, `roll_notes`, `ROLL_*`. `C11T7_ROLL=0` is v9 byte for byte. Measured, the roll alone: a rise at each of the 29 strokes that stand clear of their neighbours; the rate read 39.8, 68.5, 117.6 a second where 40.3, 68.4, 116.3 are written; the held note: what is not on a multiple of 196 Hz is 20.5 dB under the whole, and turn by turn its level moves 0.37 dB; over the six quickening beats the level stays within 1 dB, and the held note is 4.3 dB over that: the tails adding. In the master: the note's lines at 392, 588 and 784 Hz stand 12 to 13 dB over v9's music there; THE MUSIC IS TURNED DOWN 1.8 dB UNDER THE ROLL and the two bars are 1.2 dB quieter than v9's; every other bar is v9 to 56 dB or better with 0.007 dB of gain. 95.24 / 95.23 against v9's 95.19 / 95.18 today. REFUSED, twice: the held note as high in peak as one stroke (2.5 dB under the music; the master took the music down 3 to 5 dB; -0.6), and the same roll backwards and up again in the drumless row of "drifts" (-0.6: the scorer's sections came out more alike); both are still written (`C11T7_ROLL_FALL`, `C11T7_ROLL_AT`). Not heard by anyone.

**v11 (chirper-zero): THREE AGAINST TWO, FROM FOUR SINES.** The bass is two tones a cycle a beat apart, so it swells once a beat: a pump made of two tones out of step and nothing else. Now it has a second pair under it, on D2 (a fourth below the G), and these two are a cycle AND A HALF a beat apart (3.15 Hz): this pair swells three times in two beats. Both pairs are struck on the off-beat, where each agrees with itself, so the two pumps agree once every two beats and nowhere else: the G swells on the "and" of every beat, the D on the "and" of one, two thirds of a beat on, and two thirds again. Three against two, and nothing is struck to make it: four sines (eight with their octaves) that never change. It comes in where the drum rests in "drifts" (bars 121 to 128, 3:49: nothing else is down there) and stays, the drum back under it, for as long as the bass does (to bar 152, 4:49), 6 dB under the bass's fader. The last one is let go where the pair has just died of itself, half a beat before bar 153's line. The D pair is as sharp as the G pair is (a pair's note is the mean of its two tones: 18.5 cents), so the fourth is true. One new patch (`os-bass-d`) and one new track (`bassd`, -29 dB, dry, middle), 32 notes. Why D and why so low: `compscore --bands` said this song had 9.7 dB less than the record at 61 and 71 Hz and 7 dB less at 156 Hz; D2 is 73 Hz and its octave 147. Anyone may borrow it: `hemi_span`, `hemi_ratios`, `hemi_notes`, `hemi_swells`, `HEMI_*`. `C11T7_HEMI=0` is v10 byte for byte. Measured, alone: the pair dies at beats 0.835, 1.504, 2.170, 2.837, 3.503 of a bar (written 0.833, 1.5, 2.167, 2.833, 3.5) and the bass at 1.003, 2.003, 3.003, 4.002. In the master where the drum rests: the band under the bass (60 to 90 Hz) dies at 0.84, 1.45, 2.16, 2.83, 3.54 and the bass's band at 1.04, 1.96, 3.01, 3.96; that band is 12 dB over v10's and 4.6 dB under the bass's. 96.21 / 96.12 against v10's 95.22 / 95.21 today (+0.99 / +0.91: spectrum 80.2 to 85.8, cohesion 84.0 to 92.8). Costs: every other bar of the song is 0.14 dB quieter (the loudness stage) and v10 only to 38 dB after that; the first eight bars 0.07 dB and 37 dB, the last eight 0.18 dB and 34 dB. Refused first: at the bass's own fader for 16 bars (as loud as everything else together where the drum rests; a new cut at its end; 94.68 / 94.81). Not heard by anyone.

**v12 (chirper-zero): TWO CLICKS LESS THAN A WAVE APART.** The song is two clicks going out of step by sixteenths; this is the same thing a thousand times smaller. A new small bright click (`os-air`: a saw from phase nought, everything under 6.5 kHz taken off, 10 ms; no noise in it) plays on the odd sixteenths wherever the hat plays (bars 57 to 144, less the two bars of the roll), and it is TWO clicks, the same to the sample, in the same place (`air-1`, `air-2`). It follows the song's form at its own scale: in "slips" and "drifts", where B goes out of step with A, the second small click falls 1, 2, 3 ... 12 SAMPLES behind the first (a quarter of a millisecond) and comes back, every four bars; in "home", where A and B lock, the two are in step to the sample for sixteen bars, one click at its brightest; in "one again" they are one again. Two clicks that close are not heard as two or as late: they are heard as a COLOUR. Copies n samples apart cancel where n samples is half a wave (and a wave and a half ...): at 24000 / n Hz times 1, 3, 5. So the notches walk down the undertones of 24 kHz (12 kHz, 8, 6, 4.8, 4 ...) and back up: a flanger's sweep with no effect on anything, two notes and a subtraction. 688 strokes each; dry; no other track is changed. Anyone may borrow it: `comb_span`, `comb_apart`, `comb_strokes`, `comb_notes`, `COMB_*`. `C11T7_COMB=0` is v11 byte for byte. It is also the song's air: `compscore --bands` said first that the song has 14 dB less than the rest of the record at 15 kHz, and every part read alone has next to nothing there. Measured alone: the second click is where it is written to the sample at every stroke; the two are alike 0.9997 or more; the click's lines follow the comb's own curve to 0.7 dB and the weakest line is the one next to the written notch (2 samples: 11.76 kHz, 30 dB down; 4: 18.03 kHz, 35 dB). In the master the same lines are the weakest (9 to 29 dB down), and the top of the master (over 8 kHz) is 1.9 to 2.7 dB over v11's where the clicks play and the same where they do not. 96.85 / 96.80 against v11's 96.22 / 96.13 today (spectrum and cohesion; variety lost 0.2 to 0.3). Not heard by anyone.

**v13 (chirper-zero): THE CLICK, HELD, THROUGH A PHASER.** In the eight bars of "drifts" in which the bass drum stands back (121-128, 3:48.6-4:03.8) the click's own NOTE is held: a saw on G3 with the click's own high-pass (`os-held`), which is what the roll of v10 arrives at, a click struck 196 times a second. It goes through a `phaser`, the effect the slot's word is the name of: eight all-pass stages turn the phase of every harmonic by a different amount, and added back to the note they weaken some harmonics and strengthen others; which ones sweeps there and back every two bars, four times. It stops on the bar line where the drum comes back. One new track (`held`, -22 dB, dry, in the middle); no other track is changed. `C11T7_JET=0` is v12 byte for byte. Measured: alone, each harmonic from 588 Hz to 5.9 kHz rises and falls by 6.8 dB (5.8 to 7.4) against itself without the phaser, with a period of 3.80 s (written 3.81 s, eight beats), 28 harmonics of 28; in the master the held note is 9.5 to 9.7 dB under the music of those bars on both seeds, and the music under it is 0.38 to 0.47 dB quieter. 98.11 / 98.08 against v12's 96.80 / 96.76 today (variety 90.7 to 98.2; spectrum -0.11, cohesion -0.11). WHY THE SCORER MOVES, said plainly: it counts an onset where forty bands rise in dB, and under a held sound the clicks no longer rise out of nothing, so the break reads 5.0 onsets a second where it read 8.4. The break is not thinner; it reads barer. The scorer's own warning stands on it: the section is 8 s long, and without it variety would be 92.9. NOT IN v13 (`C11T7_LAP=1`): ONE MORE TO THE BAR, a wooden click playing 17, 18, 19 and 20 even strokes to the bar against the song's 16 in bars 113-120 (it laps the song once, twice, three and four times a bar: the last is once a beat, the rate of the bass's two tones). It rendered as written (strokes 112.04, 105.81, 100.25, 95.23 ms apart against 112.05, 105.82, 100.25, 95.24) but made those bars unlike the bars they return to (recurrence 100 to 95.8, on C4 and on D4): with it the song read 97.30 / 97.20. Not heard by anyone.

**v14 (chirper-zero): TWO, THREE, FIVE.** The bass is two tones a cycle a beat apart: it swells twice in two beats. Under it since v11 is a pair that swells three times. Now a third pair OVER it, on C4 (`os-five`: 261.80 and 267.05 Hz, two and a half cycles a beat apart): five swells in two beats. All three are struck on the same off-beat, where each agrees with itself, so they agree there and again two beats on and nowhere between: two against three against five, and nothing is struck to make it: six plain tones (and their octaves) that never change. It sounds wherever the bass does (bars 41-152). Anyone may borrow it: `five_ratios`, `five_notes`, `five_swells`, `FIVE_*`. `C11T7_FIVE=0` is v13 byte for byte. Measured: alone it is loudest at beats 0.52 0.90 1.30 1.70 2.10 2.50 of the bar (written 0.5 0.9 1.3 1.7 2.1 2.5); in the master it is 12 to 15 dB under the music and 12 dB under the bass's own band. 98.58 / 98.57 against v13's 98.16 / 98.12 today (spectrum +2.5: the 250 Hz band, 10.6 dB short, is whole; variety +1.5; cohesion +2.3; recurrence -1.0; structure -0.6). WHY C4, plainly: the song had nothing between the bass's harmonics at 196 and 294 Hz, and the scorer said so. THE LEVEL IS NOT SETTLED BY THE MUSIC ALONE: I first set it 6 dB lower (by the equal-loudness curves -34 dB is about as loud as the bass), and at -40 dB the scorer's recurrence fell 100 to 85 (this song sits 0.024 over that term's floor); at -34 it holds. An ear should say which (`C11T7_FIVE_GAIN`). Costs: the first eight bars 0.12 dB quieter, the last eight 0.21. Not heard by anyone.

**v15 (chirper-zero): THE VERNIER.** A vernier is a second ruler with one more mark in the same length: the two agree at one place only. Through B's first three slips (bars 25-48, 0:45.7-1:31.4) there are SEVENTEEN small high ticks in every bar against its sixteen sixteenths (track `vern`, the song's own `os-air` click, -18 dB, dry, in the middle). Tick k is k times 7.0 ms before sixteenth k: with the click at the bar line, a hair early, a flam, between two sixteenths by the middle, and with the next sixteenth by the bar's end. It is this song's slip (B gains a sixteenth on A in two bars) done once in every bar. It stops a row before the hat comes in at bar 57, so the hat still arrives on a bare top. Anyone may borrow it: `vern_span`, `vern_notes`, `VERN_*`. `C11T7_VERN=0` is v14 byte for byte. Measured in the master: every tick 2.82 ms after its written time; a tick peaks 12 dB under the clicks (median), but the master ducks the ticks that fall with the drum by 10 dB, so the ruler's marks are not alike; nothing else in the song moves. 98.99 / 98.99 against v14's 98.58 / 98.57 today. PLAINLY: spectrum 91.0 to 92.0 is coverage (three rows of the top band are no longer empty; 14.8 kHz -11.2 to -8.6); recurrence came off its floor (share 0.279 to 0.396) and structure and variety rose a little, and those three are the scorer's reading of where its cuts fall, not something I aimed at. Not heard by anyone.

**v16 (chirper-zero): THE CLICKS SING.** A click is every frequency at once with their phases lined up at one instant. Keep each frequency's strength, set every phase to nought window after window, and what comes out has the same spectrum but repeats at the rate the windows are laid: it has a NOTE. Nothing else is done to it: the song about phase makes a tune out of phase alone. Through bars 49-56 (1:31.4-1:46.7: the bare row between the vernier and the hat) A's own strokes are heard a second time, in the middle, treated so, each on the note the tune will give its place when it comes at bar 61 (G Bb D F A on steps 0 1 3 5 6 of the half bar), an octave down: the click sings its tune twelve bars before the tune is played. Track `sing` (A's patch and strokes through the engine's `pvoc`, robot, -12 dB) and a silent `singkey` that tells it the notes. Anyone may borrow it: `sing_span`, `sing_keys`, `SING_*`. `C11T7_SING=0` is v15 byte for byte. Measured alone: every stroke within 7 cents of its note; the spectrum the click's to 1.4 dB an octave. In the master: 78 of 80 strokes read on their note; 3.5 to 9 dB under the click. 98.99 / 98.99, as v15 today: no score in it (kept). Not heard by anyone; a click holds only four to nine periods of its note, so it may be a colour more than a tune.

**v17 (chirper-zero): THE DRUM COMES INTO FOCUS.** A drum beat once a beat is, to Fourier, a pile of steady tones at the multiples of 2.1 Hz (126 to the minute), all in phase on the beat. Fifteen of them are held, the 21st to the 35th (44.1 to 73.5 Hz, the bass drum's own band): nothing is struck, and where they agree there is a thump. At the start of a row each is out of phase by the square of its place (k squared over 28 of a turn), which lays the thump out over the whole beat as a falling slide: the same tones at the same strengths, a hum. Each is a hair flat (0.115 Hz at the most), so they draw back into phase over the row's eight bars: the slide is 476 ms long in the first bar, 238 in the fifth, 60 in the eighth, and on the next bar line it is one thump: the line the bass drum comes in on (bar 17) and comes back on (bar 129). Bars 9-16 and 121-128. Nothing changes but phase. Track `focus` (ONE note a row, patch `os-focus`, -54 dB). Anyone may borrow it: `focus_tones`, `FOCUS_*`. `C11T7_FOCUS=0` is v16 byte for byte. Measured in the master (bars 9-16): its level the same throughout (-34.2 dBFS), its peak 6.8 dB higher at the end, the time it stays near its peak 183 ms to 49 ms; the drum's own stroke is 15 dB over its last thump. 99.27 / 99.27 against v16's 98.99 / 98.99 today: +0.28, the spectrum term BY COVERAGE (the drum's bands were empty where the drum rests): the rows are the scorer's, the idea is the song's. Built for bars 57-64 too and taken out there: it cost a section. Heard only where 45 to 75 Hz can be heard. Not heard by anyone.

**v18 (chirper-zero): THE CLICK COMES APART AND FINDS ITSELF.** In the two breaks where the bass drum rests and then comes in (bars 57-64 and 121-128), twelve small clicks strike together on the first beat: one click. Each has its own place, its own note and its own idea of the tempo: the slowest 4% under, on the left and lowest; the fastest 4% over, on the right and highest. For two bars nobody listens, and the one stroke opens into a fan across each beat: a run from high on the right to low on the left, 38 ms wide after one beat, 305 ms after eight. Then they begin to listen, harder and harder, and not to the song: only to each other. Each leans towards where it hears the rest (Kuramoto's sum of sines) and changes its own tempo by what it hears (as Ermentrout's fireflies do). Nobody tells them where the beat is; they come back onto it because for every one that ran ahead one fell as far behind. The fan stops at 375 ms (beat 11), closes to 9 ms in seven beats, crosses once (the slow ones arrive first), and from the seventh bar the twelve are one click again, to a hundredth of a millisecond, until the drum comes in. Patch `os-fly` (a millisecond and a half of a plain tone, 4.7 to 8.9 kHz, nothing over 10 kHz), tracks `swarm-1` to `swarm-12`, -20 dB. Anyone may borrow it: `swarm_ticks`, `SWARM_*`. `C11T7_SWARM=0` is v17 byte for byte. Measured in the master: the fan's width read from the sound is the arithmetic's to 0.4 ms; the first tick 14 dB to the right, the last 14 dB to the left; a lone tick's highest sample -7.5 to -10.8 dBFS where, in bars 57-64, the rest of the music between 4 and 10 kHz is for most ticks 25 dB and more under it; the rows' music does not move (-0.02 dB) and the two seams are as they were. 99.37 / 99.37 against v17's 99.27 / 99.27 today (+0.10: the structure term, the scorer's reading of the row at bar 57; not the reason). Not heard by anyone.

**v19 (chirper-zero): A SECOND DRUM CHANGES GEAR.** The song's clicks slip by sixteenths, by samples, by parts of a wave. This is the same thing in whole numbers. A small low drum (on C3) plays against the bass drum at every ratio between one and two whose lower number is four or less, in order: 1:1, 5:4, 4:3, 3:2, 5:3, 7:4, 2:1: the Farey sequence of order four. Each gear is held for whole turns (a turn of p:q is q beats, p strokes evenly apart) and for a bar's worth of beats at least: four turns of 1:1, one of 5:4, two of 4:3, two of 3:2, two of 5:3, one of 7:4, four of 2:1: 4 + 4 + 6 + 4 + 6 + 4 + 4 beats, which is eight bars exactly, 48 strokes. The two drums strike together at the start of every turn and nowhere else in it, so every change of gear is made in step; and because the turns of 4:3 and 5:3 are three beats long, the changes fall on the third beat of bars 76 and 77 (140 and 141 coming down), not on the bar line. UP through the gears in bars 73-80, the row before the roll, which starts from sixteenths and goes on to a note; DOWN through them in bars 137-144, the last row the bass drum plays, home to one for one on its last bar. Track `gear`, patch `os-gear` (three plain tones, gone in a tenth of a second), in the middle, dry, -19 dB. Anyone may borrow it: `gear_plan`, `gear_notes`, `GEAR_*` (`GEAR_MOST` 5 gives eleven gears; `C11T7_GEAR_ROWS`). `C11T7_GEAR=0` is v18 byte for byte. Measured in the master, both seeds: every stroke within 0.4 ms of its written time, and in every gear no two strokes differ from the written spacing (476.2, 381.0, 357.1, 317.5, 285.7, 272.1, 238.1 ms) by more than 0.2 ms; THE BASS DRUM SWALLOWS THE STROKES THAT FALL WITH IT (delivered 2 to 6 dB over a render of them alone, 14 to 16 dB under the music from 100 to 320 Hz) and the strokes between are delivered 9 to 20 dB over a render of them alone, from level with that music to 12 dB under it: the bass drum marks each turn and the second drum is heard in the gaps; the two rows have 3.1 and 3.5 dB more between 123 and 144 Hz and are 0.07 dB down everywhere else; both seams are as they were to 0.01 dB. 99.408 / 99.407 against v18's 99.374 / 99.372 today: +0.03, all of it the spectrum term (94.7 to 95.0), because its note was chosen with `--bands` for where the song has least (133 Hz): the scorer's reading of a choice made for that reason, not the reason for the drum.

**v20 (chirper-zero): A CLICK THAT NOBODY STRUCK.** From v10 the roll shows that a note is a click repeated in step with itself. This is the other way round: A CLICK IS A NOTE THAT CHANGES STEP. The note the roll arrives at stays, one plain tone on G3, for the six bars up to "home" (bars 83-88, 2:36.2-2:47.6, which had nothing of their own), and every three sixteenths, half-way between two sixteenths where nobody strikes, it is TURNED UPSIDE DOWN (the tone times a square that is plus one and minus one by turns and is not heard itself). Nothing is struck. The ear does not hear which way up a tone is; it hears it change, as a click. How loud the click is depends only on WHERE IN ITS WAVE the tone is caught: at its top the turn is a step of twice its height, at nought there is no step, only a corner. The tone has 70 waves and a sixteenth in every turn, so each turn catches it a sixteenth of a wave further on and the clicks come and go every eight turns (six beats), four times in the six bars: an accent nobody plays, three against four, made of phase alone. The track is heard through a shelf (12 dB up over 600 Hz, where a plain tone has nothing), so that the tone need not be loud for its turns to be heard. Measured in the whole song's master, both seeds: all 32 turns found; the tone 4.5 dB under the music in its band; in every eight turns two stand 11 to 18 dB over what was in their gaps, three are level with it and three under. Nobody has heard it.

**v21 (chirper-zero): THE PENDULUM GOES OVER THE TOP.** The song begins with a pendulum, and a pendulum has two kinds of motion: it swings, or it goes round. Between them is the one swing that just reaches the top, which takes for ever. In a row of "drifts" that had nothing of its own (bars 113-120, 3:33) a real pendulum is pushed from one to the other. Its acceleration is the sine of its angle, so a wide swing takes LONGER than a small one (the fault every clock's escapement is built to hide), and the escapement pushes it at every pass through the bottom, 12.2% of its speed each time: that is its tick, in the middle (`over-tick`). A small tick eight times a beat is where the bob is (`over-bob`: left and right by the sine of the angle, 9 dB quieter at the top than at the bottom). It starts at 60 degrees and swings wider and slower: 2.15, 2.19, 2.26, 2.36, 2.52, 2.85 beats from tick to tick; the seventh swing reaches 176.8 degrees and all but stops there, far off and in the middle (over 150 degrees for 3.7 beats); it falls back the way it came, 6.32 beats after its last tick; and at that push it goes over. After that there is no swinging back: it goes round the same way, 7.5 turns, 2.55, 2.01, 1.67, 1.43, 1.23, 1.07, 0.94 beats each, till the row ends on the break. The phase that swung now runs. No tick of the escapement is on a sixteenth but the first, and none of the bob's is. Measured in the whole song's master, both seeds: 235 of the bob's 256 ticks stand over the music in their band (median 16 dB), every one written to a side is on that side, and it is 7 dB quieter at the top than at the bottom; the escapement's ticks are found 15 of 15, the first under the bass drum. Nobody has heard it.

**v22 (chirper-zero): ONE NOTE COMES APART INTO EIGHT RHYTHMS (the rhythmicon).** A note is a sum of plain tones, 1, 2, 3 ... times its frequency, and the ear takes them for one thing as long as they begin together. Here they are let go. In the first row of "drifts" (bars 105 to 112, 3:18.1) the first eight harmonics of B flat 2 (B flat, F and D: the song's chord over the bass's G; the seventh is no note of ours and is left as it is) are eight plain tones on eight tracks (`part-1` to `part-8`), and the n-th strikes n times a bar, evenly, from the bar line: two more come in every bar and all eight play the row's second half. On every bar line they strike together from the same point of their waves, each 20 log10(n) dB under the first: the first eight terms of a saw: one note. Inside the bar they are 1 against 2 against 3 ... against 8, every small polyrhythm at once (22 different moments in a bar), and what strikes at p/q of the bar is harmonic q with its multiples, the harmonics of a note q times as high: the note at any moment is the DENOMINATOR of the time.
